# Who she is — evidence for the avatar

## What this is and its limit

This merges three readings of 2,970 quotes from people who posted on Reddit between April and October 2026 or reviewed an app; the corpus also includes five book-review quotes. They were collected for a different question, mainly what hurts, what parents want and what they say about products, rather than to describe a representative buyer. Counts are counts of quotes, never of people, and never measures of how common something is. Multiple excerpts, replies, crossposts and repeated text can describe one event, and the app/book reviews extend from 2014 to 2026.

## What the quotes cannot tell us

These are excerpt-level limits, using the stored quote and context unless explicitly marked otherwise. A fact recovered from an original is not copied into every excerpt from that author.

| What remains unknown | Count and limit |
|---|---|
| Her typical age | **5** quote/context pairs give maternal age or age band: four exact-age cues and one “mid twenties”; two describe the same 27-year-old mother. **2,965** do not. The readers found additional ages in originals, including older mothers; there is no basis for a typical buyer age. |
| Her usual work or leave arrangement | The readers found **103 / 92 / 45** work-role cues in parts 01/02/03. These are **240** broadly coded records, but the third definition includes study and household employment; they are not a uniform count of employed mothers. The other **2,730** lack a cue under those respective definitions. No employment rate, typical job, working hours or usual leave entitlement follows. |
| Her income | **0** stored quote/context pairs state a numeric personal or household earnings amount. Prices, budgets, benefits, a single income and expensive toys establish no income bracket. Reader 03 recovered earnings/wage details in originals attached to dv23-0062 and dv24-0104; those source-only examples are not a household-income census. |
| Her education | **0** stored quote/context pairs name a completed qualification. **5** contain explicit study/exam/student cues, including one father: dv02-0241, dv03-0114, dv04-0176, dv23-0025, dv23-0203. Two more discuss their own school experience, dv21-0040 and dv21-0159. Degrees and college histories recovered in originals remain individual disclosures, never an inferred education level from a professional title. |
| Her country and market | **At least 12** records have explicit named-place ties accepted here; four are excerpts of the same Wales account. “From Belgium” can indicate origin, and South Florida names a school visit, so even these are not twelve independent residence declarations. Health-system references, currencies, storefronts, language and subreddit membership do not establish country; several UK context labels were unsupported when Reader 01 checked the originals. |
| Her family size and relationship status as a market profile | There are **284** first/only-child or novice-reference records and **292** sibling/multiple/pregnancy records in the readers’ original pattern indexes; these overlap and are not a parity census. **0** representative household rosters or relationship-status surveys were collected. A partner’s existence says nothing by itself about legal marriage, co-residence or usable help. |
| Whether she is the buyer, and what she normally spends | **0** records are linked to an identified Mamivio customer. The **159** app-review and **5** book-review records are not 164 verified buyers; trials, gifts, use, payment and shopping intentions differ. **0** systematic maps of development spending were collected before choosing these reviews. |
| A typical day, media habit, or prevalence of any difficulty | **0** representative time-use diaries or probability-sampled respondent records. Posting after bedtime, during a contact nap or at 3:42am is an individual disclosure, not a usual search hour. Neither a diagnosis nor the incidence of distress, disability, regret or harmful behaviour can be estimated from these selected excerpts. |

<details>
<summary>Ids behind the sparse age and named-place counts</summary>

Age/age band: dv02-0240 dv02-0242 dv05-0174 dv05-0184 dv21-0011.

Named-place ties: dv02-0306 dv02-0307 dv02-0308 dv02-0309 dv03-0257 dv04-0061 dv21-0051 dv21-0066 dv21-0219 dv22-0142 dv23-0036 dv23-0094.

</details>

**How to read the counts.** Each pattern gives the union of its readers’ supporting-id sets, deduplicated by id within that pattern. These are broad topic indexes: an id need not establish every narrower example or endorse the same rule as another id. Patterns overlap, their totals must not be added, and repeated accounts with different ids remain separate records. A few illustrative ids were additionally assigned here when merging; source-note and counterexample ids are kept separately. Any other-voice and no-adult-context figures beside a count are included in that index, not added to it.

**Whose account it is.** The portrait statements use what mothers and unspecified-gender parents say about themselves; unspecified parents are not automatically women. Reviewers’ own product experiences are labelled as reviews, and their family roles are used only when explicitly stated. Other speakers and excerpts with no additional adult self-context remain in the indexes, separately labelled, and cannot establish her biography. The final speaker section explains those limits.

All three readings are covered: **1,043 + 992 + 935 = 2,970** records; none remains unread in those readings. This merge does not claim a fresh reading of all underlying posts: the niche README says about **4,300 candidate Reddit posts remain unread**, and material outside the selected corpus remains outside this page. This is the evidence layer for writing an avatar; the product brief’s proposed mother, price ladder and age-selected guide are assumptions, not findings.

## Her situation

### First babies

First babies bring little reference for what is normal.

**Supporting quotes: 284** (parts 01/02/03: 76 / 173 / 35; other voices 1, no adult context 0).

The first-time wording and lack of infant reference are explicit. They do not establish that most buyers are first-time mothers.

[dv01-0137]
> I’m a first time Mum and it’s difficult to not think that I’m doing things wrong sometimes, so this is really helpful to hear ❤️

[dv05-0023]
> For context I’m an only child that didn’t grow up around younger relatives, so the early life stages are brand new to me. On top of that, I have few people in my life with babies right now

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Experience is also present, and can coexist with being dismissed as a novice:

[dv21-0080]
> I brought up developmental concerns at nearly every doctor’s appointment starting around 2 months, but I was repeatedly reassured that I was just an anxious first-time mom—even though this is my second child and I’m an occupational therapist. I consistently felt something wasn’t right, but kept being told to wait and that he’d catch up.

### Siblings and pregnancy

Other children and another pregnancy divide attention without removing uncertainty.

**Supporting quotes: 292** (parts 01/02/03: 87 / 89 / 116; other voices 6, no adult context 0).

The index includes older children, stepchildren, twins and a further pregnancy or plans for one. A long gap between children can mean learning the stage again.

[dv05-0151]
> he’s been having such a hard time with the transition to big brother that I’ve been focusing on him so much I guess I haven’t had the 1x1 time to focus and practice milestones with her like I did with my first.

[dv03-0267]
> Now with my second? Energy is gone […] But I just have no mental capacity to plan and execute fun things anymore.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** A sibling household can also have a content, independently occupied toddler; divided attention is not universal misery:

[dv25-0124]
> I am a SAHM to a 3yr girl and 18mo boy. My son is a very agreeable, easygoing kid, has a meltdown or screams occasionally but otherwise does his own thing, is very sweet to me, naps great, etc.

### Work and leave

Paid work, leave, study and staying home create different care arrangements.

**Supporting quotes: 342** (parts 01/02/03: 103 / 203 / 36; other voices 9, no adult context 0).

The statements include leaving work, returning at different ages, remote work, study and an at-home role. Return dates are individual circumstances, not national leave rules; daycare alone is not proof of employment.

[dv03-0083]
> I work full time 8 am to 4:30 pm and I dont know when I’m supposed to find time for all this???

[dv01-0103]
> I'm losing my mind being the baby cruise director all day long and am wondering if leaving my job to stay home with him full time until he turns 1 was a bad idea.

[dv25-0223]
> He is lagging behind in lifting his head on the floor and i am so scared when i go back to work my husband isnt going to do tummy time with him during the wake windows during the day. My husband is working from home for 1 month and then we will be putting the LO in daycare which is a whole other can of anxiety worms.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Some can exchange care with a hands-on partner. This is a self-identified first-time mother with a wife, not a father inferred from the partner label:

[dv06-0156]
> I know part of the reason that we’re going to try so hard for no screen time is privilege - my wife is on mat leave for a year, I work part time and from home, we’re both very hands on and can switch in and out as the other one needs a break.

### Home and getting out

Space, transport and weather limit what is practical.

**Supporting quotes: 244** (parts 01/02/03: 36 / 86 / 122; other voices 3, no adult context 0).

Small flats, shared homes, pets, one-car arrangements, weather and a baby's intolerance of transport appear across the readings. None measures income.

[dv04-0061]
> We live in a small flat in London so not a lot of space for activities... What are things that we can do in a big city like London to help him release some of his energy during autumn/winter?

[dv03-0233]
> All the parks nearby have wood chips so obviously I can’t leave my little one on the ground I basically have to be carrying him the whole time while my toddler plays.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Other households have a usable yard and money allocated to it; cramped housing is not the uniform setting:

[dv21-0029]
> We have about 3k budget left for the yard which could all go towards kid things really. Everything else is done.

### Money and value

Room to spend varies, and value matters at both ends.

**Supporting quotes: 205** (parts 01/02/03: 100 / 50 / 55; other voices 4, no adult context 0).

These are stated limits, spending and value judgments. The kit review is a report of past spending, not a current price or proof of affluence.

[dv01-0008]
> Just wondering if anyone has any recommendation for toys that are actually worth it as funds are pretty low due to SMP lol

[dvr1-0092]
> I ordered 4 years of play kits. That’s about $1,920 our one income home has given to this company on our very limited budget.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** An author also explicitly says she can afford more help from equipment:

[dv03-0107]
> I also am lucky enough to have the means and will get a robot vacuum/mop situation. I know it’s probably not necessary but it will help my dumb brain feel less anxious about it.

### Health and recovery

The baby's health and the parent's body can organize the day.

**Supporting quotes: 523** (parts 01/02/03: 76 / 162 / 285; other voices 3, no adult context 0).

Prematurity and corrected age, NICU stays, infant conditions, pregnancy, recovery and pain are concrete circumstances where stated. Worry, a diagnostic suspicion and a reported diagnosis remain different.

[dv21-0074]
> Is it 4-6 months chronological? Or 4-6 months corrected? He’s still pretty small(like 10% for weight and 2% for height). He isn’t able to hold his head up yet. I know he has some catching up to do.

> baby wearing is hard for me for anything more than 30-45 mins due to back issues and I feel like such a failure hearing how much it’s praised, because I can’t do it.

[dv22-0001]
> Her physician is not worried about her development, as she is achieving all milestones age-appropriately.

### Place, language and services

Some accounts name different places, languages and routes to care.

**Supporting quotes: 33** (parts 01/02/03: 5 / 28 / 0; other voices 2, no adult context 0).

The indexed evidence includes multilingual homes, expat experiences and references to different services. Reader 02's source check for dv21-0136 describes three household languages; the displayed quote establishes the cost of language-matched care, not her nationality.

[dv04-0061]
> We live in a small flat in London so not a lot of space for activities... What are things that we can do in a big city like London to help him release some of his energy during autumn/winter?

[dv21-0136]
> We are waiting for an autism evaluation, but they told me the wait times are 8-12 months right now. I can't afford SLP at 300$/hr (that's what I was quoted for our language) or occupation therapy.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Other accounts leave the country unnamed, and their claims about available services remain attributed beliefs:

[dv04-0137]
> There is no EI in my country and called a private PT once , he cried and protested the whole thing and I paid 100 usd for nothing

## Her day

### The repeated care round

Brief activities and basic care fill long days with few free minutes.

**Supporting quotes: 1226** (parts 01/02/03: 333 / 338 / 555; other voices 21, no adult context 0).

Feeding, changing, books, floor time, carrying and settling are already work. Long time to fill can coexist with only minutes in which to eat; nursing/pumping constraints do not establish a universal feeding choice.

[dv02-0306]
> Some days I have the energy for entertainment (passing him things on the playmat, rolling him, sitting him up on me, carrying him around, talking, singing, reading etc.) But nothing is holding his focus for very long so it just feels like a never ending conveyor belt

[dv04-0013]
> Success: got a 1and half hour nap, got to eat a sandwich, got the floor swept

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Some children do occupy themselves and nap well, making a different kind of day possible:

[dv25-0124]
> I am a SAHM to a 3yr girl and 18mo boy. My son is a very agreeable, easygoing kid, has a meltdown or screams occasionally but otherwise does his own thing, is very sweet to me, naps great, etc.

### Sleep and usable time

Naps and exhaustion determine usable time.

**Supporting quotes: 530** (parts 01/02/03: 113 / 248 / 169; other voices 18, no adult context 0).

A nap can be contact care, pumping, housework or the parent's own sleep rather than a free work block. A change in infant sleep can undo a previously workable routine.

[dv04-0165]
> I feel like my day always consists of pumping, baby care, playing with baby, and I’m so tired that when she naps I will just nap, and I’ll do stuff around the house. I haven’t gotten any me time lately.

[dv22-0044]
> my baby was doing really well at sleeping through the night and independent play and was a fairly happy baby. all of a sudden he’s screaming and crying non stop.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Improvement is also reported. This parent's attribution to growth and a rigid routine is her explanation, not a causal finding:

[dv22-0137]
> Yes it must've been skills / growth related as just after this she was trying to sit up and roll and she's exceptionally vocal!
> 
> We're back down to 1 feed a night and sometimes 2 but we're getting a good 6-8 hours now! I just kept bedtime / wake ups completely rigid even if we had a split night! Hang in there x

</details>

### Outings and company

Outings give structure, but company does not guarantee relief.

**Supporting quotes: 410** (parts 01/02/03: 104 / 146 / 160; other voices 1, no adult context 0).

Walks, libraries, swimming, groups, shops and parks already feature in some routines. Frequent plans can coexist with isolation. Neither a free group nor attendance establishes a paid class purchase.

[dv01-0172]
> I’m also finding it harder and harder to find things to keep her occupied that aren’t outside activities. She goes to gymnastics with dad every week, I take her swimming, usually once a week soft play and she’s outside at least twice a day no matter what.

[dv24-0096]
> I feel isolated despite piling my weeks high with social plans, outings and appointments. She is very social and loves being out and about but can be very fussy at home, so I feel like I have to constantly entertain her or haul her out of the house. I feel exhausted and completely depleted.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** A homebody describes an established home routine and rejects the assumption that more mother groups are desirable:

[dv02-0353]
> We have about five different “zones” around the house with different toys, books, and activities that we rotate through throughout the day, plus our backyard.

[dv02-0352]
> mommy groups frankly sound like awful dick-measuring contests over developmental milestones.

### Searching and recording

A worry can turn into searching, posting and tracking.

**Supporting quotes: 422** (parts 01/02/03: 0 / 262 / 160; other voices 10, no adult context 0).

Appointments, forms, a difficult day, relatives and online videos can prompt a search for comparable experience or a next step. The readings give only scattered explicit posting times.

[dv04-0021]
> I didn't really think about it until they had me fill out his questionaire, but now I'm worried that his eye contact has decreased, he's not cooing much anymore, and he seems behind on motor skills.

[dv22-0015]
> TikTok (of course) popped up with the scariest video of infantile spasms. Had never heard of it before. It’s wrecked me. Every move my son makes I analyze and worry about.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Not every tracker search comes from fear; one mother explicitly wants a fun record:

[dv05-0045]
> Tbh I’m not a very anxious mom and was looking for apps that would more so be fun to use rather than tracking or comparing (I.e. maybe an app where I could log when baby hits specific milestones)

## Who else has a say

### The other parent

Partners share care and decisions, and can also impose standards.

**Supporting quotes: 168** (parts 01/02/03: 35 / 31 / 102; other voices 1, no adult context 0).

These are accounts of the partner's involvement or demands as told by the parent. Disputes concern teaching, language, screens, sleep, safety and the home; reports of coercion or violence remain separately attributed below.

[dv05-0074]
> He randomly quizzes me too. Like asking if I read books to her that day and making me feel guilty if I didn’t. He expects me to constantly teach her things, talk to her in multiple languages, expose her to educational stuff, etc.

[dv03-0063]
> All of a sudden yesterday my husband tells me I have to take it all down and my son now has to freely wander the house or be in his room because he’s tired of the living room having so many baby things in it….

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** A mother can praise the same partner's practical involvement while disagreeing over sleep training:

[dv23-0090]
> He is always playing with baby and doing exercises making sure he’s getting plenty of tummy time and all of that. I mean he is truly great.

### Usable help

Having a partner or people nearby does not establish usable help.

**Supporting quotes: 390** (parts 01/02/03: 71 / 158 / 161; other voices 6, no adult context 0).

The source accounts distinguish long partner shifts, absence, single motherhood, separation and widowhood. Nearby relatives can be ill or unavailable; a spouse is not automatically a spare pair of hands.

[dv01-0153]
> I’m a SAHM, no village, husband works long days. How are we entertaining kids all day?!

[dv23-0135]
> I’m raising two toddlers by myself, my husband passed away last year so by the end of the day I’m honestly just trying to get both children fed, cleaned up, and safely into bed. Now I’m worried the therapist was judging me or thinking I was a bad mom because I admitted that I don’t read to them regularly or have an elaborate bedtime routine.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Some have paid help and still feel responsible for every minute. This challenges explaining all guilt by an absence of practical support:

[dv25-0016]
> Since he’s only a year old, I’m hyper-aware that the first two years are so critical for his development. Even though the nanny is here, I don’t actually feel like I want much "me time." I just want to spend every possible moment with him to make sure he’s hitting his milestones and getting that motherly connection.

### Extended family

Family gives care, gifts, comparisons and judgments.

**Supporting quotes: 174** (parts 01/02/03: 52 / 62 / 60; other voices 8, no adult context 0).

A relative can give the parent a break and make that break emotionally costly. Other accounts describe missing a deceased mother rather than a relative who is available to advise.

[dv22-0017]
> My problem right now is that my entire family  my mother-in-law, my own mother  are constantly getting on my nerves about how I should take her to the doctor, that she is developmentally delayed, that I should get check-ups done, and that she is behind in her development.

[dv22-0018]
> Every time she is at her grandma’s now, I do get a little time for myself, but afterward I have to listen the whole time to what I should and shouldn’t do, so that I just feel the urge not to take her anywhere anymore and simply avoid all meetings, and it’s been going on like this the whole time.

[dv25-0243]
> I just feel like I have no idea what I’m doing, sleep schedules, upset stomach, bottles, weaning, sickness, milestones etc. I don’t even know where to begin learning all of this without a mother. 
> 
> Where/  how did those without a village of mothers learn to become a mother?

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Grandparents and nannies can also provide reliable, useful care:

[dv23-0163]
> I think the newborn affected him more than we initially realized. I spend most of my day with the toddler though, and the baby’s care is managed by a grandparent in the home with me or a nanny.

### Clinical authority

Professionals can reassure, contradict, refer or blame.

**Supporting quotes: 465** (parts 01/02/03: 76 / 199 / 190; other voices 14, no adult context 0).

The clinician's words are what the parent reports hearing. The mother and occupational therapist is describing her own experience, not giving a diagnosis here; no clinical or service-eligibility claim is independently established.

[dv21-0080]
> I brought up developmental concerns at nearly every doctor’s appointment starting around 2 months, but I was repeatedly reassured that I was just an anxious first-time mom—even though this is my second child and I’m an occupational therapist. I consistently felt something wasn’t right, but kept being told to wait and that he’d catch up.

[dv21-0181]
> We sit down to go over scores formally and Dr told me that “parents who are doing enough don’t need speech therapy” and that we really need to get her around children more - she mentions a weekly play group, swim classes, parks, and a tumbling class. I informed her that we do parks 2-3 days a week, swim once a week, and a weekly open play at city hall in my town. That’s when I got scolded and told “well that’s clearly not enough you need a tumbling class” - I’m not sure what world this lady is living in but stuff gets expensive!

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Treatment and referrals can also help, and parents report improvement:

[dv23-0222]
> good thing at least is that physiotherapy is already bringing some improvements that came fairly quick.

### Childcare

Childcare is also a source of developmental feedback and a decision under pressure.

**Supporting quotes: 141** (parts 01/02/03: 31 / 46 / 64; other voices 4, no adult context 0).

Daycare can observe skills, offer company and respite, or become a feared loss of care. Attendance, considering enrollment and an actual dismissal are distinct events; some parents seek care without a paid-work requirement.

[dv04-0105]
> we also try to encourage independent play, we don’t interrupt her when she’s happy on the ground, set up fun activities, take her on stroller walks, etc. But she’s just not growing out of the clinging. She goes to daycare three days a week and crawls around behind the daycare teachers all day crying to be held.

[dv04-0106]
> I am scared she’s gonna get kicked out of daycare. Please offer advice.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Another parent credits daycare and therapy with improved engagement. That is an attributed experience, not proof of which caused the change:

[dv21-0034]
> Around 22 months is when things really started to improve and I credit it a ton to the services he's in **and the fact that we put him in daycare**. He started talking and engaging more with us. He wasn't in his own little world anymore, is the best way to put it. His OT and SLP also noticed  significant improvements around this time. We started to finally feel like everything was going to be okay. I recognize what a privilege this is compared to people whose outcomes aren't as positive.

### Online voices

Online people and content can help and judge the same parent.

**Supporting quotes: 658** (parts 01/02/03: 180 / 326 / 152; other voices 16, no adult context 0).

Replies can supply a usable idea or leave the author feeling dismissed. Google, Reddit, Instagram, TikTok, Pinterest, YouTube, ads and reviews occur; their presence does not demonstrate trust or a purchase.

[dv04-0081]
> Opening the drawers to decide his own clothes is a low stakes way to give independence throughout the day. Thanks!!

[dv04-0086]
> I don’t have a problem with screens as a whole or the older slower stuff, it’s the shows and movies that will be on the entire time that are the newer fast paced “addicting” ones. I was just looking for some advice as how to navigate a whole week. A little disheartened everyone is basically telling me to suck it up.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Parents also actively resist the claims they encounter. This mother's expressed belief rejects the purchase-equals-development premise:

[dv03-0071]
> Your child will not fall behind if you don’t buy them a fancy toy. That is manipulative marketing and you shouldn’t fall for it.

## What she already follows and believes

### Frameworks and methods

Named methods and developmental frameworks become ways to interpret the child.

**Supporting quotes: 829** (parts 01/02/03: 348 / 430 / 51; other voices 16, no adult context 0).

Montessori, RIE, child-led play, toy rotation, CDC/ASQ checklists, wake windows, leaps and named media or treatments are different frameworks, not one shared philosophy. The treatment list is reported use, not recommended care; considering Doman or an app is not buying it.

[dv24-0080]
> I’ve been listening to the podcast Montessori way and have been interested by following this lifestyle but on a budget. It’s my first child and so far I try my best to implicate some of the self-regulation, independent play, screen free, independent feeding but I want to help facilitate more for the educational aspect like body parts, colours and such. Letters were getting good at as he had a mind explosion recently is way more vocal and willing to talk- I have an alphabet book and letter cards that we use for that.

[dv04-0019]
> If she doesn’t nap when the app says she should, I worry. If she wants another feed sooner than expected, I worry. It feels like there’s a “right” way to do everything, and I’m scared I’m getting it wrong.

[dv22-0074]
> We are pulling out all the stops - medication, chiropractic care, had tongue and lip tues removed, hypoallergenic formula nutrimegen, biogaia probiotics, holding upright 30 mins after feeding, gas drops, gripe water, tummy time, baths, bicycle kicks.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Some reject the premise of controlling development, and a prospective user reports conflicting claims about Doman rather than endorsing it:

[dv24-0061]
> The truth is that we don't control our children's development, we support it. We love them, encourage them, and cheer for every new skill they learn.

[dv22-0112]
> Some parents swear by it and claim their children made extraordinary progress, sharing impressive before-and-after stories and videos. Others say it is expensive, lacks scientific evidence, and is essentially a scam.

### Rules and exceptions

Rules about stimulation and screens are negotiated against the actual day.

**Supporting quotes: 1016** (parts 01/02/03: 110 / 552 / 354; other voices 37, no adult context 0).

The index contains competing standards about constant teaching, independent play, safety, feeding, screens and attachment. Screen rules differ by age, device, content and occasion; work, illness, travel, chores and exhaustion appear as reasons for exceptions. No effect of screens is established by these accounts.

[dv22-0177]
> HOW DO YOU DO IT ALL? I am finding it hard to figure out what to sacrifice and just want to be an amazing mom (I am a perfectionist st heart).

[dv05-0158]
> We are full-time working parents and maintain screen-free environment at home.

[dv01-0082]
> I’m posting this because somewhere out there is a new mum agonising over putting on some kind of YouTube (or other streaming service) for their baby so they can get stuff done or just have a moment of peace.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Others explicitly make room for ordinary exploration and a child who need not be exceptional:

[dv24-0073]
> Let him chew his toes. Let him crawl around. Let him be silly. Let him discover things at his own pace. Let him exist without needing to be exceptional already.

[dv24-0005]
> Would I rather she play with her 400 toys than sort spices? Yes. Is it hurting anyone? No. So I just let her.

## What she has already tried or bought

### Toys and equipment

Equipment is owned, gifted, tried and considered; more toys do not ensure a break.

**Supporting quotes: 1100** (parts 01/02/03: 301 / 456 / 343; other voices 25, no adult context 0).

The broad index covers completed purchases, ownership, gifts, failed trials, shopping and value questions. Lovevery and other kits, gyms, bouncers, pens, climbers, audio players, books and household objects appear; a registry or promised order is not a sale.

[dv01-0184]
> My 7 month old baby hates baby toys and only wants household items that she’s not allowed to have. [...] I’ve bought her every chew toy and every baby toy under the sun.

[dv22-0072]
> Lastly, she hates carriers. I have 4 of them an she doesn’t like any of them. I just bought a ring sling and was so hopeful…nope. Won’t last more than a couple minutes.

[dv05-0029]
> Not trying to spend a fortune, but I do want things that will last—so I’m open to spending a bit more if it’s actually worth it.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Another parent has unopened gifts and a child who still prefers a paper cup and shoe horn:

[dv23-0010]
> I still have things from Christmas and his birthday in February that we haven't even opened! THEN easter comes around and he got 5 EASTER BASKETS and none of them were from us, his parents!! his favorite toys are paper cups and a shoe horn and do you know what his favorite toys are after the random things in the Easter baskets? paper cups and a shoe horn.

### Apps and books

Apps and books are used for activities, records and reassurance, with poor fits and cancellations too.

**Supporting quotes: 159** (parts 01/02/03: 123 / 36 / 0; other voices 7, no adult context 0).

The personal reviews include activity ideas, tracking, family sharing, lost records, corrected-age problems, repetitive tasks, subscriptions and cancellation. Kinedu, BabySparks, Wonder Weeks, Pathways, Lovevery, Bloomie and others appear. The neonatal-care review's detailed tube-feeding/hospital mismatch is in Reader 01's source-only note; its stored quote is only the short line below.

[dvr1-0146]
> I was not sure what exercise I should do, how to track my wet nappies and poo but now after having this app for two days. I can interact with my baby more, exercises is my favourite part and also, milestones.

[dvr1-0093]
> I seriously have to pay $12 a month just because I’m not buying all new kits for my younger children? Where’s the sustainability in that?

[dvr1-0120]
> This app needs to be more inclusive

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Another reviewer explicitly values the app's fit to the individual child. Neither approval nor dissatisfaction proves effectiveness:

[dvr1-0030]
> the app caters directly to my child where they are in each area instead of just assuming all 10 month olds are the same, like other apps do. Best investment I've made.

### Practice and routines

Hands-on work already includes motor practice, language and sleep attempts.

**Supporting quotes: 1049** (parts 01/02/03: 0 / 624 / 425; other voices 25, no adult context 0).

The recorded efforts include tummy time, demonstrations, therapist homework, narration, books, songs, signs, carrying and attempts to settle or separate. A completed attempt, a result and a method's effectiveness are different claims.

[dv23-0020]
> I’ve tried toys. I’ve tried food. I’ve tried making it a game. I’ve tried talking nicely. I’ve tried talking more sternly. I have ignored her tantrums. I have validated her tantrums. I continue to keep the boundary so she understands the tantrums will not get her out out of trying to walk.

[dv04-0107]
> We read books together every day, talk to him constantly, and involve him in our daily life as much as possible. We let him explore nature, choose toys that encourage development, and he enjoys both playing with us and entertaining himself independently.

[dv05-0214]
> I loved baby wearing! The day I figured it out I felt like a new person being able to be a little bit more independent. Now he is like get me out of here😭

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** A parent explicitly limits how much practice can control development:

[dv24-0061]
> The truth is that we don't control our children's development, we support it. We love them, encourage them, and cheer for every new skill they learn.

### Human help and services

Classes, assessments, therapy and other human help are used, sought or blocked.

**Supporting quotes: 480** (parts 01/02/03: 74 / 212 / 194; other voices 24, no adult context 0).

The index distinguishes attended, referred, booked, waitlisted, refused, unaffordable and contemplated help. Public EI, private therapy, assessments, childcare, consultants and classes are not all purchases. The spending quote distinguishes a bill from reimbursement; the parent's screening interpretation remains attributed.

[dv21-0033]
> At 18 months my son scored a 7 on the MCHAT, for reference, 0-2 is low risk for autism, 3-7 is mid, and 8+ is high, so things escalated a bit with our pediatrician. We were referred to a developmental pediatrician, told to increase speech to 2x week, and add in OT. We were also recommended ABA therapy but we never enrolled in it. We paid $5,000 to see a private developmental pediatrician, got a new SLP (that we still use and love!), and found an OT (that we still use and love!). All of our services are private. We pay an insane total of $890 per week (but we've met our $2k out of network deductible so now it's actually now like $300/wk after reimbursement until the end of the year).

[dv21-0136]
> We are waiting for an autism evaluation, but they told me the wait times are 8-12 months right now. I can't afford SLP at 300$/hr (that's what I was quoted for our language) or occupation therapy.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Follow-ups also report improvement; a positive outcome does not identify which service caused it:

[dv21-0127]
> All on track now and way better with everything! Would and could be brave to say everything seems typical now.

## How she sees herself

### Adequacy

Doing enough can feel impossible even with experience and extensive care.

**Supporting quotes: 950** (parts 01/02/03: 267 / 521 / 162; other voices 12, no adult context 0).

The standards can conflict: independent play feels like neglect, distressed practice feels cruel, and chores displace play. Childcare or medical experience can coexist with uncertainty; the source codes cover self-blame and professional experience, not one diagnosis.

[dv02-0071]
> I was a mother/baby nurse before I had him so I knew all the baby baby tricks and things to make my life easier. I was so prepared and newborn life was easy. Now he’s bigger and I’m like wait…I have no professional skills for this🫣🥹

[dv02-0250]
> I guess since I literally don't need to be touching/comforting or entertaining her 24/7 it felt like I was neglecting something big

[dv03-0256]
> The days i just do housework and put baby on the swing or playmat by himself I feel guilty for not playing with him more. I just can’t seem to find a balance, it always feels like I’m sacrificing something.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Professional knowledge can also help a mother reject the app's judgment rather than blame the child:

[dvr1-0077]
> Fortunately, as a doctor, I’m able to recognize that this is a failing of the app, rather than a failing of my child, but a mother without as much medical knowledge could be truly alarmed.

### Comparison

Other babies and other versions of motherhood become measuring sticks.

**Supporting quotes: 473** (parts 01/02/03: 127 / 149 / 197; other voices 13, no adult context 0).

The reference may be the twin in the same room, a cousin, an older sibling, a class or a same-stage Instagram feed. An American feed does not identify the viewer's country.

[dv01-0112]
> It’s really hard as he is a twin and his twin is constantly babbling all day so i really can’t get away or avoid the comparison.

[dv03-0096]
> Urgh I am following a (too high) number of those perfect (always American) families on insta. The algorithm seemed to know my time pregnant almost to the week

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Some mothers explicitly reject the demand for exceptional performance:

[dv24-0073]
> Let him chew his toes. Let him crawl around. Let him be silly. Let him discover things at his own pace. Let him exist without needing to be exceptional already.

### Uncertainty

Monitoring and uncertainty can displace enjoying the baby.

**Supporting quotes: 621** (parts 01/02/03: 214 / 0 / 407; other voices 3, no adult context 0).

Some want a concrete explanation or relevant outcome rather than generic reassurance. Questions alone do not diagnose anxiety, and a mother's diagnostic fear is not her child's diagnosis.

[dv22-0063]
> im not in full blown convinced “he’s autistic” mode, he’s only 8 months. but god do i have theee deep feeling that may only be a fear, but it consumes me everyday as i interact with my son (which is constant)…. im constantly engaging, modeling & teaching, but constantly looking for red flags/signs.

[dv02-0342]
> I can’t tell if HV is concerned or not really as she basically said she was referring as if I am worried they will trust my mum instincts. I have raging anxiety so I have no idea if I have mum instincts or just anxiety ahah!!

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Others come to accept uncertainty and stop looking for something to fix:

[dv22-0135]
> I think this is my issue - everything has a reason and I'm desperately trying to find said "reason" and then provide a fix. Fact is - this is just developmental and theres nothing to fix!

### Pleasure and progress

Pride, pleasure and improving days coexist with the difficult ones.

**Supporting quotes: 271** (parts 01/02/03: 75 / 89 / 107; other voices 6, no adult context 0).

Purposeful communication, shared interests, successful adaptations and new movement can be sources of pleasure. The account of an improvised fort reports the mother's choice and satisfaction; it is not an assessed play recommendation.

[dv24-0132]
> today my 10 mo started saying MAMA with purpose and conviction and I just 🥹🥹🥹 Yes you precious child it’s me it’s mama c’mere 🥹🥹🥹

[dv24-0083]
> For example, my son was bored in his play pen so i flipped it upside down and turned it into a fort. He was entertained for sooo long I felt so good for finding a solution!

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Other wanted pregnancies lead to little enjoyment and expressed regret. These are different accounts, not a universal progression toward happiness:

### A self beyond care

She also describes a person with interests, work and a preferred way to live.

**Supporting quotes: 479** (parts 01/02/03: 143 / 51 / 285; other voices 5, no adult context 0).

The readers preserve stated hobbies, work, introversion, shyness, perfectionism and a wish for adult conversation. Piano, crochet, reading, art and games belong to different speakers; they are not ingredients for one invented personality.

## Experiences that need their own labels

### Distress and regret

Love can coexist with regret, depletion and self-reported mental illness.

**Supporting quotes: 720** (parts 01/02/03: 0 / 271 / 449; other voices 7, no adult context 0).

Reported depression, PPD/PPA, OCD, medication, treatment, difficulty bonding, exhaustion, anger and suicidal thoughts are kept at the speaker's level of certainty. Boredom, regret, a named condition and an intention to act must not be collapsed into each other.

[dv22-0008]
> i always dreamed to be a mother and tried so hard to get pregnant with my son and was so happy when we finally conceived. but i haven’t had hardly any joy in motherhood yet. if you told me this is the life i would’ve been living then i honestly don’t think i would’ve wanted to have children.

[dv23-0040]
> Some days I’m okay, but I often find myself totally enraged when my baby won’t stop crying, to the point I want to hurt myself and don’t want to be here anymore so it will all be over. I usually just end up sobbing while caring for LO for the rest of the day instead.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** A mother also reports improved enjoyment after treatment. The timing and her attribution do not establish treatment effectiveness:

[dv22-0035]
> Sitting here right now, I can actually say I’m happy and enjoying my baby! (I didn’t enjoy my first born until he was like 5 months old!) I don’t dread the days and I don’t even get the evening scaries anymore.

### Different severe disclosures

Disability fears, abuse, unwanted thoughts and actual harm are distinct disclosures.

**Supporting quotes: 166** (parts 01/02/03: 0 / 115 / 51; other voices 13, no adult context 0).

These records contain different disclosures: feared disability and its imagined future; pregnancy termination/adoption considerations; abuse allegations and histories; unwanted thoughts explicitly disavowed; and deliberate harmful actions. The doctor's slur is the mother's report of his wording. dv25-0037 is an unconfirmed suspicion, not established abuse; Reader 03's source notes also preserve assault-related conception and abortion considerations (dv24-0090, dv25-0045, dv25-0089), and reported domestic abuse/custody conflict (dv24-0065, dv25-0242). None is reduced to a generic need for reassurance.

[dv21-0101]
> I did everything to prevent this. I had very low stakes for termination….like I would have terminated for any sort of major anomaly, even if it was compatible with life. The fact that I did IVF to prevent passing on a genetic disease only for him to have something worse is killing me. I obviously should not have had another kid if I didn’t consider how much having a severely disabled child would mess me up. I don’t know what to do now, but I’m highly considering adoption so another family can take better care of him, because I just don’t think I’m cut out for this as a parent.

[dv22-0064]
> I’ll never forget the doctor telling me that my son “could be absolutely normal or very mentally retarded, but won’t be able to tell until he’s at least 4”.

[dv23-0107]
> I’ve been having scary intrusive thoughts because of all of this, I would never act upon them and I feel shameful having dark thoughts. I love my kids more than anything and I just wish I could do more for them.

[dv24-0035]
> The anger is otherwise constant alongside apathy, I've hurt her deliberately as a result. My team are aware.

[dv25-0037]
> I’m so terrified of falsely accusing him and destroying lives, but I’m more afraid of wrongly dismissing something and letting my daughter be abused.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** No other quote can refute an individual disclosure. What cuts against treating catastrophic expectations as every parent's outlook is an explicit acceptance of limited control:

[dv24-0061]
> The truth is that we don't control our children's development, we support it. We love them, encourage them, and cheer for every new skill they learn.

## Who is speaking who is not her

### Fathers and partner labels

Fathers and partner-labelled speakers need their own attribution.

**Supporting quotes: 129** (parts 01/02/03: 33 / 46 / 50; other voices 126, no adult context 0).

The source-code index includes fathers, non-maternal parents, reports about a mother's distress and three partner-labelled records from a self-identified first-time mother with a wife (dv06-0155, dv06-0156, dv06-0157). Thus the index is not a count of fathers. The SAHD's guilt is his self-description. dv25-0004 and dv25-0005 are versions of one father's account of an unwanted bodily response and unwanted sexual harm thoughts; his explanations are unverified and he denies intent. They are not maternal experiences or evidence he acted.

[dv25-0057]
> As a SAHD who both wants to enjoy some free time playing my video game when I'm not cleaning but also engage in play and learning with my 17m daughter,  I essentially feel guilty often that I'm not doing enough with her.

[dv25-0004]
> Life was good but within a week of her getting here I had noticed that I had gotten an N-C while we were doing tummy time skin to skin! Not an easy moment for a dad! At this point I hadn’t done the research so I had a little panic attack but I came to the conclusion that I’m okay and so is she, nobody plans to hurt her in any way so it’s fine.

[dv25-0005]
> Happy and moving on a couple more months. And then one day I got another case and then my heart nearly stopped itself when I got an intrusive thought while holding my own daughter, my one and only extremely precious daughter, whom I would protect with my life. >!I got very close to perishing at those thoughts of harming her!<.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** The partner label also contains a mother with a wife. Her source explicitly identifies her as FTM, which overrides a male inference from the label:

[dv06-0156]
> I know part of the reason that we’re going to try so hard for no screen time is privilege - my wife is on mat leave for a year, I work part time and from home, we’re both very hands on and can switch in and out as the other one needs a break.

### Other caregivers and observers

Relatives, professionals, relayed accounts and foster carers speak from different positions.

**Supporting quotes: 43** (parts 01/02/03: 14 / 5 / 24; other voices 38, no adult context 0).

The cousin helping a bereaved mother is not the mother. The pedagogue relative's underfeeding/neglect concern is second-hand and unconfirmed. The foster-parent quotations concern one seven-year placement beginning in infancy; no current baby-buyer status or gender is inferred. A sociologist's historical argument (dv24-0025) is an attributed argument, not population evidence.

[dv24-0022]
> I agree to do most of the baby care but like what do you exactly go with a 6 month old baby? I know how to feed him and know how to change his diaper (ew) but like after those stuff... What should I do with him? Should I like show him what I'm watching or something?

[dv25-0146]
> I'm scared that underfeeding a baby for 2 months would have long-term consequences 
> 
> Is the situation salvagable? Can she "catch up"? 
> 
> If they decide to only breastfeed her and she continues to underdevelop... Can I call CPS?

[dv24-0197]
> She takes her first steps, says her first words, bonds with our kids, plays with the household pets. Builds a life.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Professional and family roles do not always exclude motherhood: this nurse is explicitly also a first-time mother, speaking about her own experience:

[dvr1-0070]
> As a pediatric nurse I had a general understanding of child development but it was not straightforward during limited social interaction.

### Reviews and commercial interests

Reviews and commercial accounts do not identify an independent mother by themselves.

**Supporting quotes: 169** (parts 01/02/03: 164 / 5 / 0; other voices 14, no adult context 32).

All 164 review records remain indexed, including five book reviews. A reviewer is not automatically a mother or verified purchaser. The five TinySteps records are from one app-builder's promotional post, not five independent endorsements; a referral code also accompanies dv01-0019, dv01-0020, dv01-0021. Possible promotional intent in dv23-0208 is uncertain, not established.

[dvr1-0007]
> was told that i would get an ebook after leaving a review and emailing them after i did it.

[dv05-0055]
> I actually ended up building a little app around this whole idea (TinySteps — it just gives you one age-appropriate activity per day based on developmental science), but honestly, the list above is the core of it. You don't need an app.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** Some reviews explicitly do identify a mother and her own budget decision; that specific self-description remains evidence:

[dvr1-0132]
> as a single mom I don’t have enough income to pay $7.99 every week. It’s easier to just go without.

### Looking back

Retrospective accounts have a different time frame.

**Supporting quotes: 71** (parts 01/02/03: 5 / 21 / 45; other voices 14, no adult context 2).

There are 71 stored looking_back stage labels across the corpus (5/21/45 by share). Earlier infant experience and present older-child circumstances must stay distinct. Other out-of-window accounts include the 25-month-old in dv03-0055, the older child in dv24-0087 and pregnant/expectant speakers; stage labels are not an exact census of current buyer eligibility.

### Fiction

The explicitly fictional story supplies no lived biography.

**Supporting quotes: 1** (parts 01/02/03: 0 / 0 / 1; other voices 1, no adult context 0).

The original is explicitly titled fictitious and the record is marked fiction_not_parent_testimony. Its attorney career, earnings/luxury, family circumstances and loneliness cannot establish anyone's biography. It remains preserved and quoted as literary material.

[dv25-0179]
> I had no family in close proximity to me and often lugged my three or four-month old baby on ninety minute car rides to see people, or bundled her up to go on walks for fresh air as the weather got colder and colder. I was so desperate for adult interaction that I would feel a small sense of joy when a stranger walked by and simply said hello.

**What cuts against a uniform reading:** The narrative is not validated by a similar real account. A separately attributed mother does describe isolation in her own life:

[dv24-0096]
> I feel isolated despite piling my weeks high with social plans, outings and appointments. She is very social and loves being out and about but can be very fussy at home, so I feel like I have to constantly entertain her or haul her out of the house. I feel exhausted and completely depleted.

<details>
<summary>Further supporting ids and the readers’ complete pattern references</summary>

Additional ids in the readers’ contextual notes for this pattern: dv23-0208.

Merged reader categories (their original counts, before removing overlap): part 03, “Explicitly fictional material” (1).

</details>

**Coverage and provenance retained from the readings.** The readers marked **93** records as giving no additional adult self-context (45 / 12 / 36); their definitions vary slightly, so this is a reading disposition, not an absence-of-biography rate. They remain in the corpus and index. No subject was excluded, and no quote or original was altered.

<details>

Commercial/assisted provenance is kept without deciding it false: dv02-0013 dv02-0014 dv02-0015 dv02-0016 dv02-0017 dv02-0018 were worded with ChatGPT help according to the mother; the playroom image in dv03-0016 dv03-0017 dv03-0018 was an AI mockup, not evidence of a completed room. Bloomie launch-period reviews and several Pathways/Lovevery review clusters have unknown independence; timing alone establishes no coordination. The aunt's 3am recommendation in dvr1-0117 is not a logged mother's search time. Repeated stored text, crossposts and multiple excerpts remain quote records rather than independent testimonies.

## Beside the public figures

These are the public figures **as filed in [the statistics page](../statistics/us-first-time-mothers.md)**, kept separate from the selected quote evidence. They were not re-fetched or independently verified for this offline merge. “Agree in kind” means that the described circumstance appears in self-reports; it never means the quotes confirm a national percentage, average or causal explanation. Populations and years differ, and almost none measures first-time mothers of babies 0–24 months specifically.

| Figure in the statistics file | What the quotes say beside it |
|---|---|
| **First-birth mean age: 27.6 in 2024; 27.5 in 2023; 25.4 in 2010** (US first births). | **Say nothing about the mean or trend.** Five excerpt/context age cues cannot establish either; additional ages in originals also span different stages and speakers. |
| **1,427,020 first children among 3,628,934 births, 2024.** | **Agree in kind** that both first and later children occur; **say nothing about the national birth-order counts or proportions**. |
| **39.5% of births to unmarried mothers, 2024** (all births). | **Say nothing about this percentage.** Single, partnered, separating and widowed accounts occur; a single mother is not necessarily unmarried at birth. |
| **88.6% high-school diploma or higher; 36.7% bachelor's or higher, 2024** (all births). | **Say nothing about these levels or shares.** The stored excerpts name no completed qualification; source-only education disclosures are individual examples. |
| **Median household income $85,320, 2021** (women 15–50 reporting a recent birth, any order). | **Say nothing about the median.** There is no numeric earnings amount in the stored quote/context census. |
| **65.4% labour-force participation, 2024**, mothers with own children under 3. | **Agree in kind** about working and at-home roles; **say nothing about participation rates**. Leave, employment and household work cues are not the same measure. |
| **61.9% labour-force participation, 2024**, youngest child under 1. | **Agree in kind** about work/return-to-work with an infant (dv25-0223); **say nothing about 61.9%**. |
| **68.3% labour-force participation with under-6s versus 78.0% with youngest 6–17, 2024.** | **Say nothing about either rate or the age-group difference.** The corpus was selected for baby-development concerns. |
| **Full-time work, 2024: 76% of employed mothers with under-6s; 81% with 6–17s; 79% of employed mothers overall versus 95% of fathers.** | **Agree in kind** that both full- and part-time roles exist (dv03-0083, dv06-0156); **say nothing about these shares or the mother/father gap**. |
| **Median leave 11 weeks; 12 / 10 / 6 weeks for household incomes ≥$75,000 / $30,000–$74,999 / <$30,000; 47% took ≥12 weeks**, Pew survey published 2017, about 2016. | **Agree in kind** about differing leave lengths and constraints; **say nothing about medians or the income gradient**. Individual eight-week returns, six-month leave and longer leave are compatible with a wider distribution. |
| **2026 parent survey: returning mothers' median leave 10 weeks; 6% took ≤2 weeks; 88% returned after leave; 15% of parents employed at birth took no time off.** | **Agree in kind** that some return and others leave work; **say nothing about any of these four quantities**. No-return and long-leave accounts do not contradict the survey. |
| **27% of private-industry employees had employer paid-family-leave access, March 2023.** | **Say nothing about that rate.** SMP or a leave deadline is not a uniform measure of employer benefit access or whether a mother's whole leave was paid. |
| **Regular nonparental care for 53% of under-3s, 2024; unpaid individual care 24%, centres 18%.** | **Agree in kind** about family, nanny and centre care (dv23-0163, dv04-0105); **say nothing about the percentages or ranking**. |
| **Nearly half of under-5s received some grandparent care**, Roosevelt fact sheet dated May 2026; underlying data year unstated. | **Agree in kind** about grandparent care; **say nothing about the share**. The condition “any time” differs from regular childcare. |
| **Primary childcare 2.5 hours/day; women 3.0, men 2.0, 2024**, adults living with under-6s. | **Agree in kind** that care occupies time; **say nothing about these means or the sex gap**. “All day” supervision and overlapping chores are not a primary-activity time diary. |
| **Leisure/sports 3.0 hours/day for employed adults with under-6s versus 4.5 without under-18s, 2024.** | **Agree in kind** about curtailed personal time; **say nothing about either mean or their difference**. A five-minute break and total daily leisure measure different things. |
| **Sleep 9.08 hours/day, 2024**, women whose youngest household child is under 6, including naps and recorded sleeplessness. | **Say nothing about the mean.** Broken nights and contact naps neither confirm nor contradict this recorded-time measure. |
| **Social parenting advice: four in five parents of 0–4s use social media; most mothers do; two in five find advice quality hard to distinguish; nearly half find it very useful for ideas**, 2023 Mott poll, 614 parents. | **Agree in kind** about advice-seeking, useful ideas and doubt (dv04-0081, dv04-0086); **say nothing about any share**. Selection from Reddit makes this unsuitable for estimating online use. |
| **Smartphone ownership: 91% of women; 97% of adults 30–49, 2026** (all US adults). | **Say nothing about ownership rates.** A mentioned phone or app gives no representative denominator, and most maternal ages/countries are unknown. |
| **Screens, 2025: 82% of under-2s ever watch TV; about four in ten ever use/interact with a smartphone; 62% watch YouTube versus 45% in 2020.** | **Agree in kind** about use and deliberate non-use (dv01-0082, dv05-0158); **say nothing about the percentages or trend**. Any use, daily use, parent phone use and child device use are different measures. |
| **Nearly a quarter suspected a milestone delay; among them 63% asked healthcare, 24% childcare, 18% only online/family/friends**, 2021 Mott poll, 779 parents with a child ≤5. | **Agree in kind** about all these advice routes; **say nothing about prevalence or route shares**. Reassurance-seeking and professionally confirmed delay remain distinct. |
| **Development/learning/behaviour concern: average 55% in 2025; about a quarter very concerned; 64% very/somewhat concerned in November**, RAPID online panel, under-3 households, 5,816 responses. | **Agree in kind** about concern and its range; **say nothing about the three quantities or change over time**. Repeated excerpts are not independent responses. |
| **36.5% standardized developmental screening in the past year, ages 9–35 months, 2023–2024.** | **Agree in kind** that questionnaires and screenings occur (dv04-0021, dv21-0033); **say nothing about coverage**. An app checklist or a visit alone does not establish standardized screening. |
| **79.6% had ≥1 preventive visit, ages 0–17, 2023–2024; higher at 0–2 but no infant-specific figure filed.** | **Agree in kind** about appointments; **say nothing about visit coverage or the age comparison**. This development-focused selection particularly favours appointment stories. |
| **Annual child expenditure at ages 0–2, married couples, 2015 dollars: $12,680 at incomes $59,200–$107,400, including $2,870 childcare/education; $9,690 below that income band; $19,770 above.** | **Say nothing about these totals or income bands.** Kit, app and therapy prices are partial spending examples, not annual family budgets; 2015 dollars are not current prices. |

No quantitative disagreement is established. The quotes cannot test national figures, and the figures cannot fill a particular speaker's missing biography. The statistics page's extra interpretations about being “mid-career” or leave being “mostly unpaid” are not adopted here: an age average and benefit-access rate do not establish those circumstances for these speakers or buyers.
